# TASKS

The queue. Pick by RUN.md §4. Status: `todo` / `in_progress` / `done` / `blocked`. A task counts as done only once STATE, TASKS and LOG are updated (RUN.md §6.5).

**Standard checks** (Node 24: `export PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/node@24/bin:$PATH`):
- **ts-js:** `cd labs/ts-js && npm test` exits 0 (typecheck + vitest).
- **angular:** `cd labs/angular && npx ng build` and `npx ng test --watch=false` exit 0.
- **links gate:** until HOUSE-01 is done: `node labs/tools/check-links.mjs` reports **0 `missing anchor`** and every `missing file` target is a planned file (a SYLLABUS §1 module slug, or GLOSSARY / QUESTION-INDEX / CHEATSHEET / MOCK-INTERVIEWS / README). After HOUSE-01: `node labs/tools/check-links.mjs --planned` exits 0.

### HOUSE-01 · S · link checker "planned" mode
- Files: `labs/tools/check-links.mjs`, `labs/tools/check-links.test.mjs`.
- Inputs: SYLLABUS §1 (slugs), RUN.md §13 (root files).
- Do: add a `--planned` flag. A `missing file` whose target basename is `NN-<slug>.md` for a SYLLABUS §1 module, or one of `GLOSSARY.md`, `QUESTION-INDEX.md`, `CHEATSHEET.md`, `MOCK-INTERVIEWS.md`, `README.md`, is counted as *planned* and reported in a summary line, not as a failure. Missing anchors and any other missing file still fail. Read the slugs from SYLLABUS.md at run time (no hard-coded list). Without the flag, behavior is unchanged (the finish line needs the strict mode).
- Acceptance: `node --test labs/tools/` passes with a new test for the planned classification; `node labs/tools/check-links.mjs --planned` exits 0 on the current tree; strict mode still reports the same count as before.

### VERIFY-OUT-M0x · M · Output answers vs test assertions (x = 1, 2, 3, 4)
- Files: `modules/0x-*.md` (answer text only), `labs/ts-js/src/outputs/0x-*/` (tests only when a test is missing or wrong).
- Inputs: the module's `## Question bank`; its outputs test folder; STYLE-GUIDE §5.3, §7.3.
- Do: for every question tagged `Output`, find the test named after its ID; check the printed lines in the Short answer (and any output block in the Full explanation) equal the asserted lines, character for character. Fix the text where it disagrees (the running test wins). Where a test is missing, add one using `captureLogs`. Also check any "in the lab, X happened" prose claim has a test named after its section.
- **Formatting (resolved in VERIFY-OUT-M02):** the shared `captureLogs` now formats with `util.format`, like `console.log`, and every existing assertion in 01–04 still passed, so no answer was affected. Nothing to do here except keep using `captureLogs`.
- Also check each Output snippet in the module is the code the test runs (`console.log` ↔ `log`, TS annotations and `Reflect` spellings are acceptable differences; anything else is a mismatch).
- Also look for self-corrections or drafting residue left in answers (01 had "`equality.test.ts`… correction: in `nullish.test.ts`").
- Acceptance: a table in the LOG line counts Output questions / tests found / mismatches fixed / tests added; every Output question has a test named after its ID (`grep` check); ts-js; links gate.

### Module 09 tasks (PLAN-M09) · common rules
- Inputs for every task: `_meta/modules/M09.md` (its section, question or exercise rows, the budget, "Facts established while planning" and the owner map), STYLE-GUIDE §3–§8, QUALITY-BAR B–E, SYLLABUS §4 (09), the module skeleton, and the "Proposed FIX tasks" of REVIEW-M05..M08 (the lessons below come from them). Re-verify every fact the task uses before writing (lab > package source > official docs) and record the basis next to the claim; facts under "Not yet read" in M09.md must be read first or dropped.
- **No real browser.** Browser enforcement of CORS, `SameSite`, CSP, Trusted Types, quotas, HTTP caching and service workers is taught from cited specifications and documentation, never as a lab observation. What the labs run is Node 24 (`fetch`, local `node:http`/`node:http2` servers, `crypto`, `structuredClone`) and jsdom 30.1.1 (cookies, Web Storage); worded as "in Node" / "in jsdom" where a browser could differ. Node's `fetch` has no HTTP cache and no CORS enforcement.
- **First-time-pass rules (recurring REVIEW findings; check each before marking done):**
  - Diagram: every section's Mental model has a Mermaid diagram (type named in the plan) followed by a "What to notice" sentence (REVIEW-M05 T-2, M06 T-1, M07 T-1, M08 T-1).
  - Misconceptions: every bullet gives the correction **and why the myth exists**; a "Once true: … until …" clause only from a source actually read this session (cite or marker); otherwise only the cause. Never from memory (REVIEW-M05 T-4, M06 T-2, M07 T-3, M08 T-2).
  - Status markers only in STYLE-GUIDE §3.2 forms, at first mention; ECMAScript edition at first mention for post-ES2015 syntax in snippets; Baseline is defined once in the module intro and is never used as a stand-alone marker form (M08 T-4). No marker is claimed unless the page was read.
  - Every term is defined on first use in this module with a link to the owning module (the per-section term list is in M09.md); acronyms are expanded once (REVIEW-M05 T-6, M06 T-6, M07 T-6, M08 T-5).
  - Questions: each is linked from its section body with its own `[Q09.NN](#q09-NN)` (never only the range endpoints), and each answer links back to its section (REVIEW-M08 T-7). Exercises are linked from their sections.
  - No "mutation check" or "checked by hand" claim in the module text; every claim of the form "in the lab, X" names a test (`Section N: …`, `Q09.NN …`, `E09.N …`) that exists and asserts it exactly (REVIEW-M06 T-8, M08 T-6).
  - Callouts begin with the `> [!NOTE]` / `> [!TIP]` line; every *Coming from the backend* states "Where the analogy breaks" (REVIEW-M08 T-8).
  - No Spring snippet in this module (M09.md); if one is added anyway it carries the Spring banner and is verified against docs.spring.io for Boot 4.1.1 / Security 7.1.1.
  - Prose budget: the module must stay <= 10,000 words by `node labs/tools/module-stats.mjs` (and the QUALITY-BAR python one-liner); each task measures its part and puts the count in its LOG line; any task more than 10% over its M09.md budget cuts before finishing, by removing repeats and never a diagram, a trap's cause or a "because" (REVIEW-M05 T-9, M06 T-4, M07 T-8).
  - Do not repeat what another module owns: abort/timeout detail is module 04 §7 / Q04.24 / Q04.26; token storage matrix is 33; CSRF in SPAs is 33/42; server CORS is 41; use one sentence plus a link.
- Module file: replace only the task's own `<!-- TASK: <ID> … -->` line(s), at the end of the task, in a single edit. Never touch another task's marker.
- Labs: `labs/ts-js/src/modules/09-web-networking-storage-security/` (section claims `section-N-<slug>.test.ts`, one per SEC task that has lab claims; exercises `<name>.ts` + `<name>.test.ts`) and `labs/ts-js/src/outputs/09-web-networking-storage-security/` (one test file per QB task with Output questions, `it('Q09.NN …')`, fixtures under `fixtures/`). Tests that need DOM-ish storage or cookies start with `// @vitest-environment jsdom` (jsdom 30.1.1 is already a devDependency of `labs/ts-js`); network tests run in the default Node environment against a local server from `labs/ts-js/src/modules/09-web-networking-storage-security/support/http-server.ts` (**built once by SEC-M09-01, read-only afterwards**; a task that needs a change records it and stops rather than editing it). Servers listen on port 0 on `127.0.0.1` and are closed in `afterEach`/`afterAll`; no test depends on wall-clock time beyond generous bounds; no test needs the internet.
- Every task adds its sources under `## Module 09 · Networking, storage and browser security` in `SOURCES.md` (create it after the Module 08 section if absent) and its inline-defined terms under `## Module 09` in `_meta/GLOSSARY-TERMS.md`.
- Acceptance for every task: `cd labs/ts-js && npm test` exits 0; `node labs/tools/check-snippets.mjs . 09` and `node labs/tools/check-links.mjs --planned` exit 0; every URL in the module is in SOURCES.md; only the declared files are new or changed; the task's marker is gone; no scratch or probe file is left behind.

### Module 10 tasks (PLAN-M10) · common rules
- Inputs for every task: `_meta/modules/M10.md` (its section, question or exercise rows, the budget, "Lab decision", "Facts established while planning", the owner map), STYLE-GUIDE §3-§8, QUALITY-BAR B-E, SYLLABUS §4 (10), the module skeleton, and the "Proposed FIX tasks" of REVIEW-M05..M09 (the lessons below come from them). Re-verify every fact the task uses before writing (lab > package source > official docs/specification) and record the basis next to the claim; everything under "Not yet read" in M10.md must be read first or dropped.
- **No real browser.** Layout, `@layer`, `@media`, `@container`, nesting, `var()` resolution and stacking order are taught from cited specifications and MDN, never as a lab observation. jsdom 30.1.1 is worded "in jsdom" and runs only what M10.md lists as working; the `Section 1:` pin tests record what it does not do. Sass is a throwaway probe (observed on a date, scratch file deleted), never a committed test.
- **First-time-pass rules (recurring REVIEW findings; check each before marking done):**
  - Diagram: every section's Mental model has a Mermaid diagram (type named in M10.md) followed by a line starting `What to notice` (REVIEW-M05 T-2, M06 T-1, M07 T-1, M08 T-1, M09).
  - Misconceptions: every bullet gives the correction **and why the myth exists**; a "Once true: ... until ..." clause only from a source actually read in this task (cite or marker); otherwise only the cause. Never from memory (REVIEW-M05 T-4, M06 T-2, M07 T-3, M08 T-2, M09 T-1/T-2).
  - Status markers only in STYLE-GUIDE §3.2 forms at first mention; **Baseline is defined once** (SEC-M10-01's verification paragraph, MDN's wording) and never used as a stand-alone marker form; no Baseline or version claim unless the page was read in the task (M10.md notes two MDN pages that gave none). ECMAScript edition at first mention for post-ES2015 features in TS snippets and exercise code (REVIEW-M08 T-4, M09 T-4).
  - Every term is defined on first use in this module (the per-section term list is in M10.md) with a link to the owning module; acronyms are expanded once (CSS, BFC, CSSOM, RTL, WCAG, UA, BEM...) (REVIEW-M05 T-6, M06 T-6, M07 T-6, M08 T-5, M09 T-2).
  - Questions: each is linked from its section body with its own `[Q10.NN](#q10-NN)` (never only range endpoints), and each answer links back to its section; exercises are linked from their sections (REVIEW-M08 T-7). Forward links before the anchor exists use plain text plus `<!-- relink: #q10-NN -->` / `<!-- relink: #ex10-N -->`; each QB/EX task turns its own back into links and deletes the comments; CLOSE: `grep -c "relink:"` is 0.
  - No "mutation check" or "checked by hand" claim; every claim of the form "in the lab, X" names a test (`Section N: ...`, `Q10.NN ...`, `E10.N ...`) that exists and asserts it exactly (REVIEW-M06 T-8, M08 T-6, M09 T-5).
  - Callouts begin with the `> [!NOTE]` / `> [!TIP]` line; every *Coming from the backend* states "Where the analogy breaks" (none is required in this module) (REVIEW-M08 T-8).
  - Output questions only where a jsdom or TS test asserts the answer exactly (Q10.04, Q10.05, Q10.13, Q10.16); the answer states what jsdom computes, never what a browser would paint.
  - Exercise statements and tests: Constraints list what the model does not cover (for example no nesting `&`, no `@scope`); only behaviors the tests assert are stated as criteria (REVIEW-M09 T-5).
  - CSS and HTML blocks start with `/* Illustrative, not run */` or `/* Run in jsdom: <test name> */`; TS snippets are Complete, Excerpt or Partial per STYLE-GUIDE §7.2.
  - Prose budget: at most 10,000 words by `node labs/tools/module-stats.mjs` (and the QUALITY-BAR python one-liner); per-part budgets in M10.md leave about 1,300 words of headroom; each task measures its part and puts the count in its LOG line; any task more than 10% over budget cuts before finishing, by removing repeats and never a diagram, a trap's cause or a "because" (REVIEW-M05 T-9, M06 T-4, M07 T-8, M09 FIX-M09-03).
  - Do not repeat what another module owns (M10.md owner map): rendering cost and `will-change` are 08, shadow DOM styling is 08 §5, encapsulation and `::ng-deep` are 13, RTL is 34, animations are 35, Material tokens are 36, zoom and contrast are 11, CSP is 09 §8; use one sentence plus a link.
- Module file: replace only the task's own `<!-- TASK: <ID> ... -->` line, at the end of the task, in a single edit. Regions without their own marker (the verification paragraph, Short on time, Summary, Check your understanding, Connections) are the placeholder line under their label or heading, replaced by the task named in the placeholder. Never touch another task's marker.
- Labs: `labs/ts-js/src/modules/10-css-essentials/` and `labs/ts-js/src/outputs/10-css-essentials/` as listed in M10.md "Lab files". Tests that need CSS start with `// @vitest-environment jsdom` and use `support/dom.ts` (**built once by SEC-M10-01, read-only afterwards**; a task that needs a change records it and stops rather than editing it). No test needs the internet; no `any`.

### Module 12 tasks (PLAN-M12) · common rules
- Inputs for every task: `_meta/modules/M12.md` (its section, question or exercise rows, the budget, "Lab decision", "Facts established while planning", "Could not be read", the owner map), STYLE-GUIDE §3-§8, QUALITY-BAR B-E, SYLLABUS §4 (12), the module skeleton, the pilot `modules/17-signals.md` for Part D depth (read a section, answer or exercise of the same kind before writing, to match its depth; never copy), and the "Proposed FIX tasks" of REVIEW-M05..M11 (the lessons below come from them, the newest from REVIEW-M10 and REVIEW-M11). Re-verify every fact the task uses before writing (lab > package source > official docs/changelog) and record the basis next to the claim; every item under "Could not be read" for the task's section is read first or the claim is dropped.
- **What runs and what is documented** (M12.md "Lab decision"): runtime claims are asserted by Angular specs under `ng test` (AOT test build with `@angular/compiler` loaded) or by `labs/ts-js` tests; compiler output and template diagnostics are "observed by compiling `<file>` with `ngc` 22.2.1 on <date>" from a throwaway project in the session scratchpad that symlinks `labs/angular/node_modules` (no install), quoted in a ```` ```text ```` block, scratch deleted; build output is "observed in the lab's production build on <date>" (`ng build`, output in the ignored `dist/`); budget failures, `define` and `fileReplacements` are observed only in a throwaway copy of the workspace in the scratchpad (deleted with its `.angular/` cache). The lab's `angular.json`, `package.json` and lockfiles are never edited. HMR in a browser, Angular DevTools, `ng update` on an old project and the webpack builder's output are documented from cited sources, **never worded as observed**. No claim is worded as observed ("in the lab", "observed", "the spec shows") unless a named test asserts it or the dated throwaway run is quoted.
- **Angular code** follows STYLE-GUIDE §7.1 (standalone, `inject()`, signals, signal inputs/outputs, built-in control flow, no `changeDetection` property unless the example is about it, v22 file names, at most four injected dependencies, no `any`). Specs in `labs/angular` that bootstrap an application create their own host element in `document.body` and call `appRef.destroy()` and remove the host in `afterEach`.
- **First-time-pass rules (recurring REVIEW findings; check each before marking done):**
  - Diagram: every section's Mental model has a Mermaid diagram (type named in M12.md) followed by a line starting `What to notice`; syntax read carefully (quoted labels for anything with brackets, colons or `@`) (REVIEW-M05 T-2 … M11 T-6).
  - Misconceptions: **every** bullet gives the correction **and a cause clause (why the myth exists)**; a "Once true: … until …" clause only from a changelog, doc or package source read in this task (cite it or use a marker), never from memory (REVIEW-M05 T-4 … M10 T-1, M11).
  - Status markers only in the STYLE-GUIDE §3.2 forms at first mention, version from the `@publicApi`/`@deprecated` tag or the CHANGELOG read in the task (`APP_INITIALIZER` "from v19.0.0", `provideZonelessChangeDetection` `@publicApi 20.2`, `strictTemplates` [Changed in v22], the webpack builders only if the CLI changelog gives the version); ECMAScript edition at first mention for post-ES2015 features in snippets and exercise code (REVIEW-M08 T-4, M09 T-4, M11 T-4). No Baseline label is needed in this module; if one is used, the form is "widely available (across browsers since <MDN date>)" or "newly available since <date>", read the same day (REVIEW-M10 F-3, M11 F-3).
  - Every term is defined on first use in this module (the per-section term lists in M12.md), inline, with a link to the owning module; acronyms expanded once (CLI, AOT, JIT, HMR, SSR, ESM, DI, CI, API, JSON…) (REVIEW-M05 T-6 … M10 T-2, M11 T-2). The glossary entries in `_meta/GLOSSARY-TERMS.md` name only terms the module actually defines, with the right anchor.
  - Editions and versions: say "v22", "22.2.1", "CLI 22.0" exactly as the source does; any product version (esbuild 0.28.2, Vite 8.3.0, rolldown 1.2.8) is the installed one, read from `package.json`, and dated.
  - Questions: each is linked from its section body with its own `[Q12.NN](#q12-NN)` (never only range endpoints) and each answer links back to its section; exercises are linked from their sections. Forward links before the anchor exists use plain text plus `<!-- relink: #q12-NN -->` / `<!-- relink: #ex12-N -->`; each EX/QB task turns its own back into links and deletes the comments; CLOSE: `grep -c "relink:"` is 0 (REVIEW-M08 T-7).
  - No "mutation check" or "checked by hand" claim; every "in the lab, X" names a test (`Section N: …`, `Q12.NN …`, `E12.N …`) that exists and asserts exactly X (REVIEW-M06 T-8, M08 T-6, M09 T-5, M10 T-5: a test that re-implements the arithmetic it claims to verify does not count).
  - Callouts begin with the `> [!NOTE]` / `> [!TIP]` line; the *Framework vs platform* callouts named in M12.md are required (section 8's carries the SYLLABUS decorator checkbox); every *Coming from the backend* callout states **Where the analogy breaks**.
  - Output questions only where a spec asserts the answer exactly (Q12.15, Q12.18, Q12.24); compile-time answers (Q12.20, Q12.21) quote the dated `ngc` observation and are not tagged Output.
  - Exercise statements: Constraints list what is not covered; only asserted behaviors are criteria; the two ts-js exercises say they are models (of `@angular/build`'s budget calculator, of instruction-based rendering), not the CLI or Angular's runtime (REVIEW-M09 T-5).
  - Snippet kinds per STYLE-GUIDE §7.2: TS is Complete (with `<sub>Source:`), Excerpt (`// Excerpt of labs/…`, verbatim), Partial (`// Partial: …`) or Output; `jsonc` blocks start with `// Illustrative, not run` or `// From labs/angular/<file>`; emitted JavaScript and console output are ```` ```text ```` with an "Observed …" line above.
  - Prose budget (Part D, RUN.md §9 and QUALITY-BAR G1: the pilot's depth scaled by section and question count, no filler; the 10,000 cap of Parts A, B, C and E does not apply): the per-part budgets in M12.md come from the measured pilot (sections mean 782, question blocks mean 248 with answers mean 226, exercises mean 581) and sum to 16,690. Each task measures its part and the whole module with `node labs/tools/module-stats.mjs` **and** the QUALITY-BAR python one-liner (the tool does not flag Part D), puts both counts in its LOG line, and **cuts before finishing if more than 5% over its budget**, by removing repeats and never a diagram, a trap's cause or a "because"; being under budget is fine when the plan items are covered (depth, not filler). CLOSE-M12 finishes at most 16,700; **the ceiling after REVIEW fixes is 17,500**, and a FIX that would pass it cuts at least as much as it adds.
  - Do not repeat what another module owns (M12.md owner map): modules and tree shaking are 05, `tsc` versus esbuild and TypeScript 7 are 06, decorators and Q07.14 are 07, templates, `$any` and encapsulation are 13, standalone and `provide*` functions are 15, injectors and multi providers are 16, uninvoked signals (Q17.32) are 17, change detection and zoneless are 21, lazy routes are 24, test runners are 29, bundle analysis is 31, SSR is 32, libraries are 38, migration practice is 39, cross-framework comparisons are 40, deployment config is 44; one sentence plus a link.
- Module file: replace only the task's own `<!-- TASK: <ID> … -->` line, at the end of the task, in a single edit. The `<!-- PLACEHOLDER: … -->` lines are replaced by the task named in them. Never touch another task's marker.
- Labs: only the files M12.md "Lab files" assigns to the task. `labs/angular` specs are named `*.spec.ts` under `src/app/modules/12-angular-how-it-works/`; `labs/ts-js` tests under `src/modules/12-angular-how-it-works/`. No test needs the internet. Throwaway probes live only in the session scratchpad and are deleted before the task ends.
- Every task adds its sources under `## Module 12 · How Angular works` in `SOURCES.md` (create it after the Module 11 section if absent; package reads as `<pkg>@<version>, <file>, read <date>`) and its inline-defined terms under `## Module 12` in `_meta/GLOSSARY-TERMS.md`.
- Acceptance for every task: the module's lab tests pass (`labs/angular`: `npx ng test --watch=false --include='src/app/modules/12-angular-how-it-works/**/*.spec.ts'` and `npx tsc -p tsconfig.spec.json --noEmit`, plus `npx ng build` when Angular code changed; `labs/ts-js`: `npm test` when ts-js files changed); `node labs/tools/check-snippets.mjs . 12` and `node labs/tools/check-links.mjs --planned` exit 0; every URL in the module is in SOURCES.md; only the declared files are new or changed (`git status --short`); the task's marker is gone; no scratch or probe file is left behind; the LOG line gives the part's and the module's prose count.

### Module 13 tasks (PLAN-M13) · common rules
- Inputs for every task: `_meta/modules/M13.md` (its section, question or exercise rows, the budget, "Lab decision", "Facts established while planning", "Could not be read", the owner map), STYLE-GUIDE §3-§8, QUALITY-BAR B-E, SYLLABUS §4 (13), the module skeleton, the pilot `modules/17-signals.md` for Part D depth (read a section, answer or exercise of the same kind before writing; never copy), and the "Proposed FIX tasks" of REVIEW-M05..M12 (the lessons below come from them). Re-verify every fact the task uses before writing (lab > package source > official docs/changelog) and record the basis next to the claim; every item under "Could not be read" for the task's section is read first or the claim is dropped.
- **What runs and what is documented** (M13.md "Lab decision"): runtime claims are asserted by Angular specs under `ng test` (AOT test build, jsdom) or `labs/ts-js` tests; compiler output and diagnostics are "observed by compiling `<file>` with `ngc` 22.2.1 on <date>" from a throwaway project in the session scratchpad that symlinks `labs/angular/node_modules` (no install), quoted in a ```` ```text ```` block, scratch deleted. Tailwind, real-browser rendering, `:host-context` support and DevTools are documented from cited sources, never worded as observed. jsdom limits (no `var()` resolution, no nested CSS, AOT-reformatted CSS) are respected: tests normalize whitespace and ids. Probes that need output under `ng test` collect it and fail an assertion to print it, in a scratch copy only. The lab's `angular.json`, `package.json` and lockfiles are never edited.
- **Angular code** follows STYLE-GUIDE §7.1 (standalone, `inject()`, signals, signal inputs/outputs, built-in control flow, no `changeDetection` property unless the example is about it, v22 file names, `protected`/`readonly` per the style guide, no `any`). Specs change signals, never plain fields (M13.md F45); fixtures that need a selector to match are nested in a host component's template (F44); selectors and templates are unique per fixture.
- **First-time-pass rules:** every section has a Mermaid diagram of the type named in M13.md followed by a line starting `What to notice` (quote labels with brackets, colons or `@`); **every** Misconceptions bullet gives the correction and a cause clause, and "Once true: … until …" only from a source read in the task; status markers only in the STYLE-GUIDE §3.2 forms at first mention with the version from a tag or the CHANGELOG; ECMAScript edition at first mention for post-ES2015 syntax; every term defined on first use with a link to the owner; questions linked from their section with their own `[Q13.NN](#q13-NN)` and answers link back; forward links before the anchor exists are plain text plus `<!-- relink: #q13-NN -->` / `<!-- relink: #ex13-N -->`, turned into links by the EX/QB task that creates the target; no "checked by hand" claim, every "in the lab, X" names a test (`Section N: …`, `Q13.NN …`, `E13.N …`) asserting exactly X; callouts begin `> [!NOTE]`/`> [!TIP]`, *Framework vs platform* callouts named in M13.md are required, every *Coming from the backend* callout states **Where the analogy breaks**; Output questions only where a spec asserts the answer exactly (Q13.05, Q13.06, Q13.07, Q13.11, Q13.13, Q13.15, Q13.22, Q13.28); compile-time answers quote dated `ngc` runs and are not tagged Output; exercise Constraints list what is not covered and only asserted behaviors are criteria; the two ts-js exercises say they are models (of the expression subset, of Angular's `ShadowCss`), not Angular's parser or compiler.
- **Prose budget** (Part D: pilot depth, no filler; the 10,000 cap does not apply): per-part budgets in M13.md sum to 18,540. Each task measures its part and the whole module with `node labs/tools/module-stats.mjs` and the QUALITY-BAR python one-liner (the tool does not flag Part D), puts both counts in its LOG line, and cuts before finishing if more than 5% over its budget. Answer parts average at most 230 words per QB chunk, none above 300; exercises about 580, at most 620.
- Do not repeat what another module owns (M13.md owner map): `?.` in JavaScript is 01, `this` and listeners 02, decorators 07, Shadow DOM and events 08, cascade and custom properties 10, accessibility 11, `strictTemplates` and what a template compiles to 12, `NgClass`/`NgStyle`/pipes 14, `imports` 15, DI 16, signal inputs/`model()`/uninvoked signals 17, `@if`/`@for`/`@let` in depth 18, projection and decorator I/O 19, lifecycle 20, change detection 21, forms 25, testing 29, sanitization 33; one sentence plus a link.
- Module file: replace only the task's own `<!-- TASK: <ID> -->` line, at the end of the task, in a single edit. `<!-- PLACEHOLDER: … -->` lines are replaced by the task named in them. Never touch another task's marker. Labs: only the files M13.md "Lab files" assigns to the task, under `src/app/modules/13-components-and-templates/` (`labs/angular`) and `src/modules/13-components-and-templates/` (`labs/ts-js`). No test needs the internet. Throwaway probes live only in the session scratchpad and are deleted before the task ends.
- Every task adds its sources under `## Module 13 · Components and templates` in `SOURCES.md` (create it after the Module 12 section if absent; package reads as `<pkg>@<version>, <file>, read <date>`) and its inline-defined terms under `## Module 13` in `_meta/GLOSSARY-TERMS.md`.
- Acceptance for every task: the module's lab tests pass (`labs/angular`: `npx ng test --watch=false --include='src/app/modules/13-components-and-templates/**/*.spec.ts'` and `npx tsc -p tsconfig.spec.json --noEmit`, plus `npx ng build` when Angular code changed; `labs/ts-js`: `npx vitest run src/modules/13-components-and-templates` when ts-js files changed); `node labs/tools/check-snippets.mjs . 13` and `node labs/tools/check-links.mjs --planned` exit 0; every URL in the module is in SOURCES.md; only the declared files are new or changed (`git status --short`); the task's marker is gone; no scratch or probe file is left behind; the LOG line gives the part's and the module's prose count.

### Module 14 tasks (PLAN-M14) · common rules
- **QB budget (2026-10-08, after EX-M14-05):** the module stands at 11,362 prose words (sections and exercises ran ≈ 370 over plan). To close at most 17,780 with ≈ 800 for the closing, each QB chunk adds at most +1,000 module words for 5 questions and +800 for 4 (≈ 200 per question block including its title, Short answer, Full explanation, Follow-ups and Trap); measure before and after with the one-liner and cut before finishing if over.
- **Corrections found while writing (override M14.md):** `findLocaleData` falls back to `en` only when the language part is `en` (not "locale, prefix, `en`" for every language); `@angular/common/locales/` holds 742 `.js` files (M14.md's 2,228 counted `.map`/`.ts` too); NG2019's text ends with a period (F25); a bare attribute on a `booleanAttribute`-less input sets `''` (SEC-M14-01). QB answers use these.
- Inputs for every task: `_meta/modules/M14.md` (its section, question or exercise rows, the budget, "Lab decision", "Facts established while planning", "Could not be read", the owner map), STYLE-GUIDE §3-§8, QUALITY-BAR B-E, SYLLABUS §4 (14), the module skeleton, the pilot `modules/17-signals.md` for Part D depth (read a section, answer or exercise of the same kind before writing; never copy), and the "Proposed FIX tasks" of REVIEW-M05..M13 (the lessons below come from them). Re-verify every fact the task uses before writing (lab > package source > official docs/changelog) and record the basis next to the claim; every item under "Could not be read" for the task's section is read first or the claim is dropped.
- **What runs and what is documented** (M14.md "Lab decision"): runtime claims are asserted by Angular specs under `ng test` (AOT test build, jsdom) or `labs/ts-js` tests; compiler output and diagnostics are "observed by compiling `<file>` with `ngc` 22.2.1 on <date>" from a throwaway project in the session scratchpad that symlinks `labs/angular/node_modules` (no install), quoted in a ```` ```text ```` block, scratch deleted. SSR behavior, real-browser rendering, timings and non-`en` locale data are documented from cited sources, never worded as observed. Probes that need output under `ng test` collect it and fail an assertion to print it, in a scratch copy only. The lab's `angular.json`, `package.json` and lockfiles are never edited. TestBed error cases use `rethrowApplicationErrors: false` only where M14.md says so.
- **Angular code** follows STYLE-GUIDE §7.1 (standalone, `inject()`, signals, signal inputs/outputs, built-in control flow, no `changeDetection` property unless the example is about it, v22 file names, `protected`/`readonly` per the style guide, no `any`). Specs change signals, never plain fields; fixtures that need a selector to match are nested in a host component's template; selectors and pipe names are unique per fixture (`lab-`/`lab` prefix); a class used by `ngComponentOutlet` is exposed as a member (M14.md F38).
- **First-time-pass rules:** every section has a Mermaid diagram of the type named in M14.md followed by a line starting `What to notice` (quote labels with brackets, colons or `@`); **every** Misconceptions bullet gives the correction and a cause clause, and "Once true: … until …" only from a source read in the task; status markers only in the STYLE-GUIDE §3.2 forms at first mention with the version from a tag or the CHANGELOG; every term defined on first use with a link to the owner; questions linked from their section with their own `[Q14.NN](#q14-NN)` and answers link back; forward links before the anchor exists are plain text plus `<!-- relink: #q14-NN -->` / `<!-- relink: #ex14-N -->`, turned into links by the EX/QB task that creates the target; no "checked by hand" claim, every "in the lab, X" names a test (`Section N: …`, `Q14.NN …`, `E14.N …`) asserting exactly X; callouts begin `> [!NOTE]`/`> [!TIP]`, *Framework vs platform* callouts named in M14.md are required, every *Coming from the backend* callout states **Where the analogy breaks**; Output questions only where a spec asserts the answer exactly (Q14.03, Q14.04, Q14.10, Q14.15, Q14.18, Q14.20, Q14.22, Q14.25, Q14.27); compile-time answers quote dated `ngc` runs and are not tagged Output; exercise Constraints list what is not covered and only asserted behaviors are criteria; the ts-js exercise says it is a model of `AsyncPipe`, not Angular's pipe; no "SYLLABUS" in reader text.
- **Prose budget** (Part D: pilot depth, no filler; the 10,000 cap does not apply): per-part budgets in M14.md sum to 17,770. Each task measures its part and the whole module with `node labs/tools/module-stats.mjs` and the QUALITY-BAR python one-liner (the tool does not flag Part D), puts both counts in its LOG line, and cuts before finishing if more than 5% over its budget. Answer parts average at most 230 words per QB chunk, none above 300; exercises about 580, at most 620.
- Do not repeat what another module owns (M14.md "Owned elsewhere"): `host` metadata, reference variables and `exportAs` use 13, `strictTemplates` and what a template compiles to 12, signals, `computed` versus method in general and signal inputs 17, DI 16, `imports`/NgModules 15, `@if`/`@for`/`@switch` and the control-flow migration in depth 18, projection 19, lifecycle 20, change detection 21, i18n and `registerLocaleData` 34, forms directives 25, SSR 32, sanitization 33; one sentence plus a link.
- Module file: replace only the task's own `<!-- TASK: <ID> -->` line, at the end of the task, in a single edit. `<!-- PLACEHOLDER: … -->` lines are replaced by the task named in them. Never touch another task's marker. Labs: only the files M14.md "Lab files" assigns to the task, under `src/app/modules/14-directives-and-pipes/` (`labs/angular`) and `src/modules/14-directives-and-pipes/` (`labs/ts-js`). No test needs the internet. Throwaway probes live only in the session scratchpad and are deleted before the task ends.
- Every task adds its sources under `## Module 14 · Directives and pipes` in `SOURCES.md` (create it after the Module 13 section if absent; package reads as `<pkg>@<version>, <file>, read <date>`) and its inline-defined terms under `## Module 14` in `_meta/GLOSSARY-TERMS.md`.
- Acceptance for every task: the module's lab tests pass (`labs/angular`: `npx ng test --watch=false --include='src/app/modules/14-directives-and-pipes/**/*.spec.ts'` and `npx tsc -p tsconfig.spec.json --noEmit`, plus `npx ng build` when Angular code changed; `labs/ts-js`: `npx vitest run src/modules/14-directives-and-pipes` when ts-js files changed); `node labs/tools/check-snippets.mjs . 14` and `node labs/tools/check-links.mjs --planned` exit 0; every URL in the module is in SOURCES.md; only the declared files are new or changed (`git status --short`); the task's marker is gone; no scratch or probe file is left behind; the LOG line gives the part's and the module's prose count.

### Module 15 tasks (PLAN-M15) · common rules
- Inputs for every task: `_meta/modules/M15.md` (its section, question or exercise rows, the budget, "Lab decision", "Facts established while planning", "Could not be read", the owner map), STYLE-GUIDE §3-§8, QUALITY-BAR B-E, SYLLABUS §4 (15), the module skeleton, the pilot `modules/17-signals.md` and the finished `modules/16-dependency-injection.md` for Part D depth (read a section, answer or exercise of the same kind before writing; never copy), and the "Proposed FIX tasks" of REVIEW-M12..M16 (the lessons below come from them). Re-verify every fact the task uses before writing (lab > package source > official docs/changelog) and record the basis next to the claim; every item under "Could not be read" for the task's section is read first or the claim is dropped.
- **What runs and what is documented** (M15.md "Lab decision"): runtime claims are asserted by Angular specs under `ng test` (AOT test build, jsdom) or the `labs/ts-js` test of Exercise 15.4; compiler output and diagnostics are "observed by compiling `<file>` with `ngc` 22.2.1 on <date>" from a throwaway project in the session scratchpad that symlinks `labs/angular/node_modules` (no install), quoted in a ```` ```text ```` block; schematic runs are "observed by running the schematic on a scratch workspace on <date>" (lab config files copied, `node_modules` symlinked, snapshots compared with `diff -ru`, never git), quoted as ```` ```diff ````/```` ```text ```` blocks; scratch deleted. Bundle size, performance, SSR bootstrap and the Angular team's rationale are documented from cited, dated sources, never worded as observed. Probes that need output under `ng test` collect it and fail an assertion to print it, in a scratch copy only. The lab's `angular.json`, `package.json` and lockfiles are never edited (in particular the `test` target gets no `tsConfig` option: F27 is shown in a scratch workspace).
- **Corrections to apply from the plan (they override anything older):** **NgModules are not deprecated** (F1: no tag): never write `[Deprecated]` on `NgModule`, `RouterModule` or `forRoot`; quote the docs' "recommends … for all new code" instead (F12). `HttpClientModule` is `[Deprecated since v18.0 — use provideHttpClient(withInterceptorsFromDi())]` and the animations modules `[Deprecated since v20.2 — use animate.enter or animate.leave]` (F6, F13). The standalone default marker is exactly "[Changed in v19.0: `standalone` defaults to `true`]" (the form modules 13 and 14 use). No "stable since" or "developer preview" for standalone without the read of C1. Two docs statements failed when compiled (F12, C6): the overview's `providers: [CustomMenuModule.forChild()]` (TS2322) and "bootstrap components are automatically declared" (no compile scope): state the observation, not the docs' sentence as fact. The migration page's "unit tests are not AoT compiled" is stale for the v22 builder (C5). `ModuleWithProviders` without a generic is "deprecated" per the typings with no version: no marker. Removed APIs (`entryComponents`, `NgModuleFactory`, `createNgModuleRef`, `ComponentFactoryResolver`) appear only as non-compiling history (SYLLABUS §5), never in labs. All `ɵ` names are internal: read for understanding, never recommended to application code (Q12.25).
- **Angular code** follows STYLE-GUIDE §7.1 (standalone, `inject()`, signals, signal inputs/outputs, built-in control flow, no `changeDetection` property unless the example is about it, v22 file names, `protected`/`readonly` per the style guide, no `any`) and RUN.md §10 (at most 4 injected dependencies per class); new services use `@Service()` unless the example is about `@Injectable`, module `providers` or constructor injection. **Legacy code** (NgModules, `standalone: false`, `*ngFor`, `CommonModule`) lives only under a `legacy/` folder or a `#### Legacy` heading and says what it is; every `standalone: false` class is declared by an NgModule in the same compilation, and every spec that renders one imports that module (F26: TestBed `declarations` do not give a component its AOT scope). Fixtures use unique selectors, token descriptions and class names (`lab-m15-` prefix): NgModule declarations are global to the compilation (`NG6007`). Specs change signals, never plain fields; a jsdom bootstrap spec creates and removes its host element and destroys what it bootstrapped.
- **First-time-pass rules:** every section has a Mermaid diagram of the type named in M15.md followed by a line starting `What to notice` (quote labels with brackets, colons, `@`, `*` or parentheses: REVIEW-M14 F-2); **every** Misconceptions bullet gives the correction and a cause clause, and "Once true: … until …" only from a source read in the task; status markers only in the STYLE-GUIDE §3.2 forms at first mention, plain (not backticked), with the version from a tag or the CHANGELOG lines of M15.md F13 (no other version is stated); every term defined on first use with a link to the owner (REVIEW-M14 T-4 and REVIEW-M16 T-3 named `TestBed`, OnPush, zoneless, `ngOnDestroy`, signal: define or link them here too); each section with a legacy form has a `#### Legacy` subsection under `### Code` with the migration path (REVIEW-M16 T-9c); "How it actually works" answers at least one "why does it behave like that" from a source read (REVIEW-M16 T-1), marked "read from the 22.2.1 source" where it is; questions linked from their section with their own `[Q15.NN](#q15-NN)` and answers link back (the anchors already exist in the skeleton, so links to questions and exercises are plain links from the start; use `<!-- relink: … -->` only for a target that is not in the file yet); no "checked by hand" claim, every "in the lab, X" names a test (`Section N: …`, `Q15.NN …`, `E15.N …`) asserting exactly X, and a test title never claims more than its assertions (REVIEW-M14 F-5); callouts begin `> [!NOTE]`/`> [!TIP]`/`> [!WARNING]`, every *Coming from the backend* callout states **Where the analogy breaks**; Output questions only where a spec asserts the answer exactly (Q15.04, Q15.08, Q15.13, Q15.17); compile-time answers quote dated `ngc` runs and are not tagged Output; exercise Constraints list what is not covered and only asserted behaviors are criteria; the ts-js exercise says it is a model of compilation scope, not Angular's compiler; no "SYLLABUS" in reader text.
- **Marker regions:** a section's region is its marker line plus the six empty `###` headings under it. A question chunk's region is its marker line plus the question anchors and headings that follow it up to the next marker (QB-M15-01 also writes the bank intro at its placeholder; the headings and anchors stay as in the skeleton and the question text goes under them). An exercise's region is its marker line, its `<a id>` and its heading (EX-M15-01 also writes the exercise intro at its placeholder). CLOSE-M15's marker is in `## Summary`; the placeholders for the header's Short on time line, Check your understanding and Connections name CLOSE-M15, and the header's Labs line carries a relink comment for CLOSE-M15.
- **Prose budget** (Part D: pilot depth, no filler; the 10,000 cap does not apply): per-part budgets in M15.md sum to 16,540. Each task measures its part and the whole module with `node labs/tools/module-stats.mjs` and the QUALITY-BAR python one-liner (the tool does not flag Part D), puts both counts in its LOG line, and cuts before finishing if more than 5% over its budget. Answer parts average at most 230 words per QB chunk, none above 300; exercises about 580, at most 620. Each QB chunk adds at most about +1,250 module words for 5 questions (≈ 250 per question block); measure before and after. The skeleton measured 1,445 words by `module-stats.mjs` on 2026-10-08 (headings, question titles and placeholders count); if SEC-M15-08 ends with the sections more than 3% over 6,850, it writes EX/QB caps into this block (as SEC-M16-09 did) so the module still closes at 16,550. **Measured by SEC-M15-08 (2026-10-08):** sections 7,028 (+178, +2.6%, under the 3% trigger), but the header, Contents and verification paragraph measure 435 against 300 (the Short-on-time line is still to come), so at budget the module would close near 16,890. **Caps to close at 16,550:** each QB chunk at most +1,180 module words (answer parts averaging at most 215), each exercise at most 570 prose words (none above 600); with these the module lands near 16,520.
- Do not repeat what another module owns (M15.md "Owned elsewhere"): the bootstrap walk, `createApplication`, initializers, locality, `ɵcmp` anatomy and the linker 12; component anatomy, `NG8001` and `NG8113` 13; standalone pipes, host directives and control flow replacing `NgIf` 14; `EnvironmentProviders`, `NG0207`, `provideX()` design, `providedIn: NgModule`, route injector identities and TestBed overrides 16; `@defer` 18; zoneless 21; routing 24; HTTP 27; TestBed in depth 29; SSR 32; Elements 37; folder architecture 38; whole-codebase migration strategy 39; one sentence plus a link.
- Module file: replace only the task's own `<!-- TASK: <ID> -->` region, at the end of the task, in a single edit. `<!-- PLACEHOLDER: … -->` lines are replaced by the task named in them. Never touch another task's marker. Labs: only the files M15.md "Lab files" assigns to the task, under `labs/angular/src/app/modules/15-standalone-and-ngmodules/` (`labs/angular`) and `labs/ts-js/src/modules/15-standalone-and-ngmodules/` (`labs/ts-js`). ### Module 18 tasks (PLAN-M18) · common rules
- Inputs for every task: `_meta/modules/M18.md` (its section, question or exercise rows, the budget, "Lab decision", "Facts established while planning", "Could not be read", the owner map), STYLE-GUIDE §3-§8, QUALITY-BAR B-E, SYLLABUS §4 (18), the module skeleton, the pilot `modules/17-signals.md` and the finished `modules/15-standalone-and-ngmodules.md` and `modules/16-dependency-injection.md` for Part D depth (read a section, answer or exercise of the same kind before writing; never copy), and the "Proposed FIX tasks" of REVIEW-M12..M16 (the lessons below come from them). Re-verify every fact the task uses before writing (lab > package source > official docs/changelog) and record the basis next to the claim; every item under "Could not be read" for the task's section is read first or the claim is dropped.
- **What runs and what is documented** (M18.md "Lab decision"): runtime claims are asserted by Angular specs under `ng test` (AOT test build, jsdom) or the `labs/ts-js` test of Exercise 18.4; compiler output and diagnostics are "observed by compiling `<file>` with `ngc` 22.2.1 on <date>" from a throwaway project in the session scratchpad that symlinks `labs/angular/node_modules` (no install), quoted in a ```` ```text ```` block; the schematic run is "observed by running the schematic on a scratch workspace on <date>" (lab config files copied, `node_modules` symlinked, snapshots compared with `diff -ru`, never git), quoted as ```` ```diff ````/```` ```text ````; the lazy-chunk list is "observed by `ng build` of a scratch workspace on <date>", observed and not explained. Scratch only under `mktemp -d` outside the repo, deleted at the end. Server behavior, HMR, performance and the Angular team's rationale are documented from cited, dated sources, never worded as observed. Probes that need output under `ng test` collect it and fail an assertion to print it, in a scratch copy only. The lab's `angular.json`, `package.json` and lockfiles are never edited.
- **Corrections to apply from the plan (they override anything older):** no rationale or performance claim for control flow (C1: the blog is unreadable); `@defer` has no stable-since marker (C2), say "added in 17.0.0"; control flow `[Stable since v18.0]` only with the 17.3.12/18.0.0 docs diff cited (C2) and only at its first mention in the module; `@let` is `[Stable since v19.0]` (added 18.1.0); `@default never;` `[Added in v21.2]` and `@default never(expr)` `[Added in v22.0]`; `on idle(timeout)` and `provideIdleServiceWith` `[Added in v22.0]`; viewport options `[Added in v21.0]`; `as` on `@else if` `[Added in v20.2]` (all CHANGELOG, F20); `@boundary` is `[Developer preview since v22.2]` and owned by 37 (one clause); incremental hydration default `[Changed in v22.0: …]` (documented, 32 owns it). `deferredImports` and keyed `@defer` are not public and are not taught (C8). The typings comment "Defaults to `manual`" for `deferBlockBehavior` is stale against the runtime constant and the docs: state the runtime (C7). The docs' barrel-file warning did not reproduce: state the observation against the docs, not the docs' sentence as fact (C5). The docs list only `mouseover` and `focusin` for hover, the source has three events (C6). Every `ɵ` name is internal: read for understanding, never recommended (Q12.25). The CHANGELOG lists 22.2.2 while VERSIONS pins 22.2.1: write against 22.2.1 (C10).
- **Angular code** follows STYLE-GUIDE §7.1 (standalone, `inject()`, signals, signal inputs/outputs, built-in control flow, no `changeDetection` property unless the example is about it, v22 file names, `protected`/`readonly` per the style guide, no `any`) and RUN.md §10 (at most 4 injected dependencies per class). **Legacy code** (`*ngIf`, `*ngFor`, `ngSwitch`, `NgIf` imports) lives only under a `legacy/` folder or a `#### Legacy` heading and says what it is, and compiles under `ng build`. Fixtures (modules loaded by `@defer`) use unique selectors and class names (`lab-m18-` prefix) and are standalone; a fixture with a top-level `await` or `throw` is built by `ng build` before a spec relies on it. Specs change signals, never plain fields; a spec that uses real timers uses wide margins and asserts ordering and windows, not exact milliseconds; `IntersectionObserver` is stubbed on `globalThis` and restored in `afterEach`.
- **First-time-pass rules (from REVIEW-M14, M15 and M16, so the module needs no FIX for them):**
  - Mermaid: every section has a diagram of the type named in M18.md followed by a line starting `What to notice`; quote every label that has brackets, colons, `@`, `*` or parentheses (almost every label in this module: `@if`, `@for`, `@defer`); parse each diagram with the mermaid parser before finishing (REVIEW-M14 F-2).
  - Misconceptions: **every** bullet gives the correction **and a cause clause** (why the myth exists); a place or a date is not a cause (REVIEW-M15 F-4); "Once true: … until …" only from a source read in the task.
  - Status markers: only in the STYLE-GUIDE §3.2 forms at the **first mention in the module** (check the first body mention, not the section's own heading; REVIEW-M15 T-5), plain, not backticked, with the version from a tag or CHANGELOG line of M18.md F20; no other version is stated.
  - Terms: define at first use with an owner link: `TestBed` (module 29), AOT and JIT (Q12.19), zoneless and OnPush (21), signal (17), `ngOnDestroy`, facade, barrel, adapter, `ErrorHandler`, `PendingTask`, `IntersectionObserver` (REVIEW-M14 T-4, M15 T-1, M16 T-3); run a first-use script over the module before finishing.
  - Code: each `### Code` subsection has numbered steps (QUALITY-BAR B5); each section with a legacy form has a `#### Legacy` subsection under `### Code` with the migration path (sections 1, 2, 3 and 8).
  - Mechanism: "How it actually works" answers at least one "why does it behave like that" from a source read, marked "read from the 22.2.1 source" with the file and function named, and "read, not run" where no test asserts it (REVIEW-M16 T-1, F-1 of M15); never state a behavior from emitted code that a test does not assert (REVIEW-M15 F-1).
  - Evidence: no "checked by hand" claim; every "in the lab, X" names a test (`Section N: …`, `Q18.NN …`, `E18.N …`) asserting exactly X, and a test title never claims more than its assertions (REVIEW-M14 F-5); every quoted runtime or compiler text keeps its leading underscore or explains it (REVIEW-M15 F-3); a docs sentence that failed when compiled or ran differently is worded as an observation against the docs (M15 C5 and C6 precedent).
  - Titles: a question title counts what the answer gives (a title promising three causes gets three answers: REVIEW-M15 T-4); Output only where a spec asserts the answer exactly, compile-time answers quote dated `ngc` runs and are not tagged Output.
  - Links: questions linked from their section with their own `[Q18.NN](#q18-NN)` (never only range endpoints) and answers link back; links to other modules carry a section or question anchor, not the bare file (REVIEW-M15 T-6); `<!-- relink: … -->` only for a target that is not in the file yet; callouts begin `> [!NOTE]`/`> [!TIP]`/`> [!WARNING]`, every *Coming from the backend* callout states **Where the analogy breaks**.
  - Format: a blank line before every `###` heading and before a list that follows a bold label (no list glued to `**Full explanation.**`; REVIEW-M16 T-8); no doubled commas; no "SYLLABUS" in reader text; exercise Constraints list what is not covered and only asserted behaviors are criteria; the ts-js exercise says it is a model of the documented rules, not Angular's runtime.
  - SOURCES: every URL and every package read gets a line in `SOURCES.md`, **including code-source lines for each exercise and `q18-outputs.spec.ts` added by the EX and QB tasks** (REVIEW-M15 T-2, REVIEW-M16 T-5); a script lists URLs in the module that are not in SOURCES (must print nothing).

- **Marker regions:** a section's region is its marker line plus the six empty `###` headings under it. A question chunk's region is its marker line plus the question anchors and headings that follow it up to the next marker (QB-M18-01 also writes the bank intro at its placeholder; the headings and anchors stay as in the skeleton and the question text goes under them). An exercise's region is its marker line, its `<a id>` and its heading (EX-M18-01 also writes the exercise intro at its placeholder; SEC-M18-01 writes the verification paragraph at its placeholder). CLOSE-M18's marker is in `## Summary`; the placeholders for the header's Short on time line, Check your understanding and Connections name CLOSE-M18, and the header's Labs line carries a relink comment for CLOSE-M18.

### Module 19 tasks (PLAN-M19) · common rules
- Inputs for every task: `_meta/modules/M19.md` (its section, question or exercise rows, the budget, "Lab decision", "Facts established while planning" F1-F22, "Could not be read" C1-C12, the owner map), STYLE-GUIDE §3-§8, QUALITY-BAR B-E, SYLLABUS §4 (19), the module skeleton, the pilot `modules/17-signals.md` and the finished `modules/16-dependency-injection.md` and `modules/18-control-flow-and-defer.md` for Part D depth (read a section, answer or exercise of the same kind before writing; never copy), and `_meta/reviews/REVIEW-M18.md`, `REVIEW-M16.md` and `REVIEW-M15.md` § Proposed FIX tasks (the lessons below come from them). Re-verify every fact the task uses before writing (lab > package source > official docs/changelog) and record the basis next to the claim; every item under "Could not be read" for the task's section is read or probed first, or the claim is dropped.
- **What runs and what is documented** (M19.md "Lab decision"): runtime claims are asserted by Angular specs under `ng test` (AOT test build, jsdom); compiler output and diagnostics are "observed by compiling `<file>` with `ngc` 22.2.1 on <date>" from a throwaway project in the session scratchpad that symlinks `labs/angular/node_modules` (no install), quoted in a ```` ```text ```` block; a schematic run is "observed by running the schematic on a scratch workspace on <date>" (lab config files copied, `node_modules` symlinked, snapshots compared with `diff -ru`, never git), quoted as ```` ```diff ````. Scratch only under `mktemp -d` outside the repo, deleted at the end. Server and hydration behavior, animations, CDK portals, performance and the Angular team's rationale are documented from cited, dated sources, never worded as observed. Probes that need output under `ng test` collect it and fail an assertion to print it, in a scratch copy only. The lab's `angular.json`, `package.json` and lockfiles are never edited. No `labs/ts-js` files in this module.
- **Corrections to apply from the plan (they override anything older):** no rationale or performance claim for signal inputs, outputs, queries or projection (C1: the blog is unreadable); signal `input()`/`output()`/`model()` and the signal queries are `[Stable since v19.0]` (CHANGELOG 19.0.0, F22), fallback content in `ng-content` is `[Added in v18.0]`, `read: Injector` is `[Added in v22.2]` only after the C4 probe or as "documented, not run", `ComponentFactoryResolver` and `ComponentFactory` are `[Removed in v22.0]`; `inputBinding`, `outputBinding` and `twoWayBinding` are `[Added in v20.0]` with **no** stable or preview claim (C2); an empty `projectableNodes` array rendering the fallback is `[Changed in v19.0: …]` (CHANGELOG, F22). Only the first body mention of each carries the marker (check the first body mention, not the section heading). `ngProjectAs` keeps its attribute: state the observation against the docs (C6). The decorators are not deprecated (F21). The CHANGELOG lists 22.2.2 while VERSIONS pins 22.2.1: write against 22.2.1 (C11). Every `ɵ` name is internal and is **marked internal at its first use in each section** (REVIEW-M18 T-2), read for understanding, never recommended (Q12.25).
- **Angular code** follows STYLE-GUIDE §7.1 (standalone, `inject()`, signals, signal inputs/outputs, built-in control flow, no `changeDetection` property unless the example is about it, v22 file names, `protected`/`readonly` per the style guide, no `any`) and RUN.md §10 (at most 4 injected dependencies per class). **Legacy code** (`@Input`, `@Output`, `@ViewChild`, `@ContentChild`, `@ContentChildren`, `ComponentFactoryResolver`) lives only under a `legacy/` folder or a `#### Legacy` heading, says what it is, and compiles under `ng build` (`ComponentFactoryResolver` is removed and appears only as text labelled removed). Fixtures use unique selectors and class names (`lab-m19-` prefix) and are standalone. Specs change signals, never plain fields. The `NG8011` warning of the intentional fixtures prints in the test build: say so once in the module (REVIEW-M18 T-7).
- **First-time-pass rules (from REVIEW-M14, M15, M16 and M18, so the module needs no FIX for them):**
  - Factual slips: "highest versus last" and "first versus last" statements are checked against the data (a version, a slot, an instance: F6, F7, F10 "last instance", F17 "first matching slot") before writing a superlative; every number in prose is traced to a test, run or read.
  - Mermaid: every section has a diagram of the type named in M19.md followed by a line starting `What to notice`; quote every label that has brackets, colons, `@`, `*`, `[` or parentheses; parse each diagram with the mermaid parser before finishing (REVIEW-M14 F-2).
  - Misconceptions: **every** bullet gives the correction **and a cause clause**; a place or a date is not a cause; "Once true: … until …" only from a source read in the task.
  - Status markers: only in the STYLE-GUIDE §3.2 forms at the **first mention in the module**, plain, not backticked, with the version of M19.md F22; no other version is stated.
  - Terms: define at first use with an owner link: `TestBed` (29), OnPush and zoneless (21), signal (17), `ngOnDestroy`, `EventEmitter`, extended diagnostic (Q12.21), `strictTemplates` (Q12.20), schematic (Q12.05), AOT and JIT (Q12.19), `ɵcmp` (Q12.24), `ApplicationRef`, `EnvironmentInjector` (Q16.29); run a first-use script over the module before finishing.
  - Code: each `### Code` subsection has numbered steps (QUALITY-BAR B5); sections 1, 6 and 7 have a `#### Legacy` subsection under `### Code` with the migration path.
  - Mechanism: "How it actually works" answers at least one "why does it behave like that" from a source read, marked "read from the 22.2.1 source" with the file and function named (each name found by `grep` in the named file), and "read, not run" where no test asserts it (REVIEW-M16 T-1); never state a behavior from emitted code that a test does not assert.
  - Evidence: no "checked by hand" claim; every "in the lab, X" names a test (`Section N: …`, `Q19.NN …`, `E19.N …`) asserting exactly X, and a test title never claims more than its assertions; every quoted runtime or compiler text keeps its leading underscore or explains it; a docs sentence that ran differently is worded as an observation against the docs.
  - Titles: a question title counts what the answer gives (Q19.02 gives three diagnostics, Q19.11 three fixes, Q19.20 three mistakes: REVIEW-M15 T-4); Output only where a spec asserts the answer exactly, compile-time answers quote dated `ngc` runs and are not tagged Output.
  - Links: questions linked from their section with their own `[Q19.NN](#q19-NN)` (never only range endpoints) and answers link back; links to other modules carry a section or question anchor, not the bare file (REVIEW-M15 T-6, REVIEW-M18 T-6); `<!-- relink: … -->` only for a target that is not in the file yet; callouts begin `> [!NOTE]`/`> [!TIP]`/`> [!WARNING]`, every *Coming from the backend* callout states **Where the analogy breaks**.
  - Format: a blank line before every `###` heading and before a list that follows a bold label; no doubled commas; no "SYLLABUS" in reader text; exercise Constraints list what is not covered and only asserted behaviors are criteria.
  - SOURCES: every URL and every package read gets a line in `SOURCES.md`, **including code-source lines for each exercise and `q19-outputs.spec.ts` added by the EX and QB tasks** (REVIEW-M15 T-2, REVIEW-M16 T-5, REVIEW-M18); a script lists URLs in the module that are not in SOURCES (must print nothing).
  - Repeats (no retelling): tell each mechanism once and link it afterwards; the slot-matching rule (§3), "projection is not instantiation" (§3), the query timing table (§5) and the schematic outcome (§6) are the four most likely to be retold in questions and in §8, and the answers link the section instead of restating it (REVIEW-M15 T-3).
- **Marker regions:** a section's region is its marker line plus the six empty `###` headings under it. A question chunk's region is its marker line plus the question anchors and headings that follow it up to the next marker (QB-M19-01 also writes the bank intro at its placeholder; the headings and anchors stay as in the skeleton and the question text goes under them). An exercise's region is its marker line, its `<a id>` and its heading (EX-M19-01 also writes the exercise intro at its placeholder; SEC-M19-01 writes the verification paragraph at its placeholder). CLOSE-M19's marker is in `## Summary`; the placeholders for the header's Short on time line, Check your understanding and Connections name CLOSE-M19, and the header's Labs line carries a relink comment for CLOSE-M19.
- **Prose budget** (Part D: pilot depth, no filler; the 10,000 cap does not apply): per-part budgets in M19.md sum to 18,260. Each task measures its part and the whole module with `node labs/tools/module-stats.mjs` and the QUALITY-BAR python one-liner, puts both counts in its LOG line, and cuts before finishing if more than 5% over its budget. The skeleton measured 1,552 words by `module-stats.mjs` on 2026-10-08 (headings, question titles and placeholders count). Answer parts average at most 230 words per QB chunk, none above 300; exercises about 580, at most 620. A QB chunk of 5 questions adds at most about +1,250 module words, a chunk of 4 at most about +1,000 (≈ 250 per question block); measure before and after. If SEC-M19-08 ends with the sections more than 3% over 7,000, it writes EX/QB caps into this block (as SEC-M18-09 did) so the module still closes at 18,270. **Caps written by main after SEC-M19-08 (2026-10-08):** the module measured 8,647 after §1-§8; 18,270 − 8,647 = 9,623 left, the closing keeps ≈ 800, so EX + QB get ≈ 8,820 (split as planned, 7,220 : 2,940): each exercise ≤ +505 module words (the EX-M19-01 intro included in its +505), each 5-question QB chunk ≤ +1,070 and the 4-question chunk ≤ +900 (answers average ≈ 200, none above 260). These replace the +1,250 / +1,000 / ≈ 580 figures above. Measure before and after; a chunk over its cap cuts repeats before saving.
- Do not repeat what another module owns (M19.md "Owned elsewhere"): signal API basics 17; `[(x)]` desugaring and `host` 13; `TemplateRef`, `ViewContainerRef`, the context guard and the outlets as directives 14; `providers`/`viewProviders` and scopes 16; control flow 18; emitted template code in general 12; hook order 20; OnPush 21; RxJS interop 22/23; router input binding 24; forms 25; state libraries 26; TestBed in depth 29; sanitization 33; animations 35; CDK portals 36; Angular Elements 37; whole-codebase migration 39; one sentence plus a link.
- Module file: replace only the task's own `<!-- TASK: <ID> -->` region, at the end of the task, in a single edit. `<!-- PLACEHOLDER: … -->` lines are replaced by the task named in them. Never touch another task's marker. Labs: only the files M19.md "Lab files" assigns to the task, under `labs/angular/src/app/modules/19-component-communication-and-projection/`. No test needs the internet. Throwaway probes live only in the session scratchpad and are deleted before the task ends.
- Every task adds its sources under `## Module 19 · Component communication and projection` in `SOURCES.md` (after the last module section present; package reads as `<pkg>@<version>, <file>, read <date>`) and its inline-defined terms under `## Module 19` in `_meta/GLOSSARY-TERMS.md`.
- Acceptance for every task: the module's lab tests pass (`labs/angular`: `npx ng test --watch=false --include='src/app/modules/19-component-communication-and-projection/**/*.spec.ts'` and `npx tsc -p tsconfig.spec.json --noEmit`, plus `npx ng build` when Angular code changed, which also compiles the `legacy/` fixtures); `node labs/tools/check-snippets.mjs . 19` and `node labs/tools/check-links.mjs --planned` exit 0; every URL in the module is in SOURCES.md; only the declared files are new or changed (`git status --short`); the task's marker is gone; no scratch or probe file is left behind; the LOG line gives the part's and the module's prose count.

### Module 20 tasks (PLAN-M20) · common rules
- Inputs for every task: `_meta/modules/M20.md` (its section, question or exercise rows, the budget, "Lab decision", "Facts established while planning" F1-F22, "Could not be read" C1-C14, the owner map), STYLE-GUIDE §3-§8, QUALITY-BAR B-E, SYLLABUS §4 (20), the module skeleton, the pilot `modules/17-signals.md` and the finished `modules/16-dependency-injection.md`, `modules/18-control-flow-and-defer.md` and `modules/19-component-communication-and-projection.md` for Part D depth (read a section, answer or exercise of the same kind before writing; never copy), and `_meta/reviews/REVIEW-M19.md` (when it exists), `REVIEW-M18.md`, `REVIEW-M16.md` and `REVIEW-M15.md` § Proposed FIX tasks (the lessons below come from them). Re-verify every fact the task uses before writing (lab > package source > official docs/changelog) and record the basis next to the claim; every item under "Could not be read" for the task's section is read or probed first, or the claim is dropped. The facts of M20.md were probed 2026-10-08 on 22.2.1; a task re-runs the ones it quotes under `ng test`.
- **What runs and what is documented** (M20.md "Lab decision"): runtime claims are asserted by Angular specs under `ng test` (AOT test build, jsdom, zoneless TestBed); diagnostics are "observed by compiling `<file>` with `ngc` 22.2.1 on <date>" from a throwaway project in the session scratchpad that symlinks `labs/angular/node_modules` (no install), quoted in a ```` ```text ```` block. Scratch only under `mktemp -d` outside the repo, deleted at the end. **Documented, never worded as observed:** server and hydration behavior of render hooks (a `PLATFORM_ID` override does not emulate it), zone.js hook frequency, production-mode absence of NG0100, `provideCheckNoChangesConfig` behavior, the Angular team's rationale, performance, `ngOnDestroy` on page unload. Probes that need output under `ng test` collect it and fail an assertion to print it, in a scratch copy only. The lab's `angular.json`, `package.json` and lockfiles are never edited. No `labs/ts-js` files in this module. In a TestBed test, wrap the component under test in a host: a root fixture component's own hooks are not re-run by a second `detectChanges` (F1).
- **Corrections to apply from the plan (they override anything older):** no rationale or performance claim (C1). `afterEveryRender` and `afterNextRender` are `[Stable since v20.0]`; `afterRender` and `AfterRenderPhase` are gone (renamed in 20.0.0; neither is exported by the 22.2.1 typings and `migrations.json` has no entry: shown as removed text only, C12); `takeUntilDestroyed` is `[Stable since v19.0]`, its completing at once on a destroyed ref is `[Changed in v20.0.1: …]` (or a plain sentence); `DestroyRef` is `[Added in v16.0]`, `DestroyRef.destroyed` `[Added in v20.1]`; `provideCheckNoChangesConfig` is `[Developer preview since v20.0]` with no behavior claim (C8); `ChangeDetectorRef.checkNoChanges` is `[Removed in v22.0]`; `SimpleChanges<T>` is `[Added in v21.0]`. Only the first body mention of each carries the marker (check the first body mention, not the section heading). **State against the docs, as observations (C10):** the lifecycle table lists `ngOnInit` before `ngOnChanges` (the run and the docs text say `ngOnChanges` first); a signal written in `ngAfterViewInit` does not throw NG0100. A plain-field write in a microtask is not taught as a fix (C6). The CHANGELOG lists 22.2.2 while VERSIONS pins 22.2.1: write against 22.2.1 (C9). Every `ɵ`, `LView` and `TView` name is internal and is **marked internal at its first use in each section**, read for understanding, never recommended (Q12.25). **`takeUntilDestroyed` is owned here** (Q20.15); 22/23 apply it (open point for Jorge).
- **Angular code** follows STYLE-GUIDE §7.1 (standalone, `inject()`, signals, signal inputs/outputs, built-in control flow, no `changeDetection` property unless the example is about it (the v22 default is OnPush; an `Eager` host is explicit and said so), v22 file names, `protected`/`readonly`, no `any`) and RUN.md §10 (at most 4 injected dependencies per class). **Legacy code** (the `Subject` + `takeUntil` + `ngOnDestroy` pattern, the broken and leaky exercise starts) lives only under a `legacy/` folder or a `#### Legacy` heading, says what it is, and compiles under `ng build`; `afterRender`, `AfterRenderPhase` and `checkNoChanges` appear only as text labelled removed. Fixtures use unique selectors and class names (`lab-m20-` prefix) and are standalone; hooks are recorded in a local array, never `console.log`. Specs change signals, never plain fields (except the fixtures that demonstrate the plain-field case).
- **First-time-pass rules (from REVIEW-M14, M15, M16, M18 and M19, so the module needs no FIX for them):**
  - Factual slips: "deepest first versus last", "first versus last" and "before versus after" statements are checked against the recorded order (F1, F2, F3, F7, F12) before writing; every number in prose is traced to a test, run or read.
  - Mermaid: every section has a diagram of the type named in M20.md followed by a line starting `What to notice`; quote every label that has brackets, colons, `@`, `*`, `[` or parentheses; parse each diagram with the mermaid parser before finishing.
  - Misconceptions: **every** bullet gives the correction **and a cause clause**; a place or a date is not a cause; "Once true: … until …" only from a source read in the task.
  - Status markers: only in the STYLE-GUIDE §3.2 forms at the **first mention in the module**, plain, not backticked, with the versions of M20.md F19/F20; no other version is stated.
  - Terms: define at first use with an owner link: change detection and OnPush and zoneless (21), signal and `effect` and `computed` (17), injection context (Q16.11), `DestroyRef` (Q16.27), `Subscription` (22), `TestBed` (29), `ApplicationRef`, declaring view (Q19.12), `LView`/`TView` (12 §8), layout thrashing (08), leak (05); run a first-use script over the module before finishing.
  - Code: each `### Code` subsection has numbered steps (QUALITY-BAR B5); sections 4, 5 and 6 have a `#### Legacy` subsection under `### Code` (and §2 a short one) as M20.md says.
  - Mechanism: "How it actually works" answers at least one "why does it behave like that" from a source read, marked "read from the 22.2.1 source" with the file and function named (each name found by `grep` in the named file, e.g. `_debug_node-chunk.mjs`), and "read, not run" where no test asserts it (REVIEW-M16 T-1); never state a behavior from emitted code that a test does not assert.
  - Evidence: no "checked by hand" claim; every "in the lab, X" names a test (`Section N: …`, `Q20.NN …`, `E20.N …`) asserting exactly X, and a test title never claims more than its assertions; every quoted runtime or compiler text keeps its `NGxxxx:` prefix; a docs sentence that ran differently is worded as an observation against the docs.
  - Titles: a question title counts what the answer gives (Q20.12 gives three mistakes, Q20.16 three leaks, Q20.21 four fixes); Output only where a spec asserts the answer exactly, compile-time answers quote dated `ngc` runs and are not tagged Output.
  - Links: questions linked from their section with their own `[Q20.NN](#q20-NN)` (never only range endpoints) and answers link back; links to other modules carry a section or question anchor, not the bare file; `<!-- relink: … -->` only for a target that is not in the file yet; callouts begin `> [!NOTE]`/`> [!TIP]`/`> [!WARNING]`, every *Coming from the backend* callout states **Where the analogy breaks**.
  - Format: a blank line before every `###` heading and before a list that follows a bold label; no doubled commas; no "SYLLABUS" in reader text; exercise Constraints list what is not covered and only asserted behaviors are criteria.
  - SOURCES: every URL and every package read gets a line in `SOURCES.md`, **including code-source lines for each exercise and `q20-outputs.spec.ts` added by the EX and QB tasks**; a script lists URLs in the module that are not in SOURCES (must print nothing).
  - Repeats (no retelling): tell each mechanism once and link it afterwards; the first-render order (§1), the render-phase order (§4), the teardown order (§5) and the NG0100 matrix (§6) are the four most likely to be retold in questions and in §7, and the answers link the section instead of restating it (REVIEW-M15 T-3).
- **Marker regions:** a section's region is its marker line plus the six empty `###` headings under it. A question chunk's region is its marker line plus the question anchors and headings that follow it up to the next marker (QB-M20-01 also writes the bank intro at its placeholder). An exercise's region is its marker line, its `<a id>` and its heading (EX-M20-01 also writes the exercise intro at its placeholder; SEC-M20-01 writes the verification paragraph at its placeholder). CLOSE-M20's marker is in `## Summary`; the placeholders for the header's Short on time line, Check your understanding and Connections name CLOSE-M20, and the header's Labs line carries a relink comment for CLOSE-M20.
- **Prose budget** (deltas over the module count measured just before the task; M20.md "Budget"): skeleton 1,349 measured 2026-10-08 by `node labs/tools/module-stats.mjs` and the QUALITY-BAR python one-liner (both give the same count); SEC deltas 1,050 / 800 / 850 / 1,000 / 850 / 850 / 850 (6,250); EX 560 (intro included) + 3 × 520 (2,120); QB 5 × 1,200 (6,000; QB-M20-01 includes the bank intro of at most 40 words); CLOSE 800. **CLOSE at most 16,520 words; REVIEW ceiling 17,420.** Each task measures before and after with both counters, puts both counts in its LOG line, and cuts before finishing if more than 5% over its delta (repeats first; never a diagram, a trap's cause or a "because"). Answer parts average at most 230 words per QB chunk, none above 300. If SEC-M20-07 ends with the sections more than 3% over 6,250, it writes EX and QB caps here so the module still closes at 16,520.
- Do not repeat what another module owns (M20.md "Owned elsewhere"): injection context, `DestroyRef` scopes and injector destroy 16; effect scheduling, cleanup, `afterRenderEffect`, `NG8118` and signal inputs 17; an output emitted in the constructor, query timing per hook, decorator setters 19; host-directive hook order and `Renderer2` 14; what marks a view dirty, OnPush, `ChangeDetectorRef`, zoneless scheduling and `provideCheckNoChangesConfig` 21; unsubscription strategies compared and leak detection 23; the router 24; TestBed in depth 29; `ngDoCheck` performance 31; SSR and hydration 32; animation hooks 35; Angular Elements 37; one sentence plus a link.
- Module file: replace only the task's own `<!-- TASK: <ID> -->` region, at the end of the task, in a single edit. `<!-- PLACEHOLDER: … -->` lines are replaced by the task named in them. Never touch another task's marker. Labs: only the files M20.md "Lab files" assigns to the task, under `labs/angular/src/app/modules/20-lifecycle-and-render-hooks/`. No test needs the internet. Throwaway probes live only in the session scratchpad and are deleted before the task ends.
- Every task adds its sources under `## Module 20 · Lifecycle and render hooks` in `SOURCES.md` (after the last module section present; package reads as `<pkg>@<version>, <file>, read <date>`) and its inline-defined terms under `## Module 20` in `_meta/GLOSSARY-TERMS.md`.
- Acceptance for every task: the module's lab tests pass (`labs/angular`: `npx ng test --watch=false --include='src/app/modules/20-lifecycle-and-render-hooks/**/*.spec.ts'` and `npx tsc -p tsconfig.spec.json --noEmit`, plus `npx ng build` when Angular code changed, which also compiles the `legacy/` fixtures); `node labs/tools/check-snippets.mjs . 20` and `node labs/tools/check-links.mjs --planned` exit 0; every URL in the module is in SOURCES.md; only the declared files are new or changed (`git status --short`); the task's marker is gone; no scratch or probe file is left behind; the LOG line gives the part's and the module's prose count.
